        {
  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 2,
    "idea": "# Sprint 2 \u2014 Multi-profile bake-off (concurrent, worktree-isolated, audit-merged)\n\n## Goal\n\nAdd a `megaplan bakeoff` subcommand that runs the same idea through N profiles **concurrently**, each inside its own git worktree, then a new comparison/selection step chooses one to keep. Final merge is **asymmetric**: code changes from one chosen profile, evaluation data from all profiles.\n\n**Prerequisite**: Sprint 1 (step receipts + canonical prompt hash + scope-drift first-class metric + global audit log). This sprint depends on receipts being in place \u2014 both for the comparison step's inputs and for the audit records that get merged back.\n\n## Why\n\nSingle-profile runs give us one data point. To answer \"which profile does this kind of task best\" and \"which model is scope-disciplined at execute\" we need side-by-side runs on identical inputs. Worktrees make the runs truly independent (no shared git state, no crossed-stream executor output). Concurrency makes the wall time bearable.\n\n## Scope\n\n### 1. Worktree lifecycle\n\n- Worktrees live **sibling to the repo**, not inside `.megaplan/`. Path: `<repo-parent>/.megaplan-worktrees/<exp-id>/<profile>/`. This avoids the circular-recursion risk where megaplan's plan-dir discovery (`rglob(\".megaplan\")`) would otherwise walk into the worktrees.\n- Each worktree is created with `git worktree add --detach <path> <base-sha>`. `base-sha` is captured once at bake-off start; every profile demonstrably starts from the same commit.\n- **Detached HEADs** are deliberate \u2014 we don't want N profile branches polluting `git branch`.\n- Cleanup is **explicit and deferred**: on successful completion, artifacts are copied back first, then `git worktree remove --force`. On crash, worktrees are left in place with a `BAKEOFF_CRASHED` marker file so forensic diffs survive. `megaplan bakeoff abandon <exp-id>` offers explicit cleanup later.\n\n### 2. Concurrent execution\n\n- The orchestrator launches N `megaplan auto` subprocesses (one per worktree, `cwd=<worktree>`) with per-profile logs redirected to `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/<profile>/auto.log`. **Do not interleave to a shared stderr** \u2014 that's unreadable at N\u22653 and destroys post-mortem value.\n- Use `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec` (not threads \u2014 signal handling is cleaner with subprocesses, and auto-driver already shells out per phase).\n- Observability:\n  - `megaplan bakeoff status [--exp <id>]` \u2014 compact table `profile | state | phase | iter | age | cost`.\n  - `megaplan bakeoff tail --exp <id> [--profile X]` \u2014 either tail one log or multiplex all with `[profile]` prefixes. Unified tail is a convenience; per-profile log files remain canonical.\n\n### 3. Comparison / selection step\n\nA **standalone subcommand**, not an auto-integrated phase. Invoked separately so it can be re-run with a different judge, resumed, or retried after the bake-off completes.\n\n`megaplan bakeoff compare --exp <id> [--judge <model>]`\n\n**Inputs** per profile:\n- `state.json` (final state, iteration counts, rework cycles, escalations)\n- All step receipts (from Sprint 1) \u2014 the primary source of structured metrics\n- Phase artifacts (`plan_v1.md`, `critique_v*.json`, `execution.json`, `review_output.json`)\n- `git diff <base-sha>..HEAD` from the worktree\n- Auto-driver's final `DriverOutcome` JSON\n\n**Decision tier** \u2014 three layers, advisory-then-authoritative:\n1. **Auto-computed metrics** \u2014 always produced. Diff stats, test pass/fail, rework cycle count, escalations, review outcome, duration, tokens, scope-drift severity per phase.\n2. **LLM judge** \u2014 configurable model (default: a model *not* present as an executor in any profile being compared \u2014 e.g., if the profiles use kimi and glm, judge with claude or gpt-5). Pairwise structured prompt: \"Given these two bundles of artifacts, rank them and flag any scope drift, quality concerns, or missed requirements.\" Advisory only.\n3. **Human pick** \u2014 `megaplan bakeoff pick --exp <id> --profile <name> [--rationale ...]` records the final selection. If the human agrees with the judge, one command.\n\n**Output**: `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/comparison.json` + a readable `comparison.md`.\n\n### 4. Comparison schema (load-bearing)\n\nThis schema is the thing that accumulates across runs into a dataset. Version it explicitly (`schema_version: 1`). Write the same fields even when a profile failed early (use nulls + flags, never missing keys).\n\nMinimum fields:\n- `experiment_id`, `base_sha`, `idea_hash` (canonical hash of the idea input)\n- `profiles[]`:\n  - `name`, `worktree_path`, `plan_id`, `outcome_status` (done / stalled / failed / escalated / aborted)\n  - `metrics`: `duration_s`, `cost_usd`, `rework_cycles`, `escalations`, `review_verdict`, `diff_lines`, `tests_added`, `scope_drift_severity_by_phase`\n  - `receipts_ref` \u2014 relative path list to the receipts that belong to this profile\n- `judge_verdict`: `{ judge_model, rank[], rationale_per_profile, scope_drift_flags, concerns[] }`\n- `human_decision`: `{ chosen_profile, rationale, decided_at }` (populated by `bakeoff pick`)\n\n### 5. Merge mechanics (asymmetric)\n\n`megaplan bakeoff merge --exp <id>` applies:\n\n- **Code half** (from one chosen worktree): `git diff <base-sha>..<chosen-worktree-HEAD>` piped to `git apply` in the main tree. Not `git merge` \u2014 we don't want throwaway-branch commit history. `git apply` fails loudly on conflicts with any main-tree work done in parallel, which is the right behavior.\n- **Evaluation half** (from *all* worktrees): copy `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/<profile>/` trees back. Paths are profile-namespaced by construction \u2014 no collision.\n- **Chosen plan dir**: the winning profile's `.megaplan/plans/<plan-id>/` is copied into the main tree's `.megaplan/plans/` so follow-up work can reference it. Losing profiles' plan dirs remain only in the bake-off archive, not in the live plan list.\n\nGotcha to solve explicitly: `state.json.config.project_dir` contains absolute paths from the worktree. The merge step either rewrites those paths to the main-tree path or marks archived plan dirs as read-only forensic data that must not be loaded by live commands.\n\n### 6. Failure & partial success\n\n- Each profile exits with a `DriverOutcome.status` in `{done, stalled, escalated, failed, aborted, cap, blocked, awaiting_human, tiebreaker_*}`.\n- The bake-off treats anything other than `done` as a non-winner candidate but **still surfaces it to compare** \u2014 a profile stalling at gate is valuable evaluation data (\"this profile can't plan this kind of idea\").\n- `megaplan bakeoff resume <exp-id>` checks each profile's state, relaunches auto only for profiles that haven't reached terminal, leaves winners alone.\n\n### 7. CLI surface\n\n```\nmegaplan bakeoff run --idea-file <path> --profiles A B C [--mode code] [--exp-id <name>]\nmegaplan bakeoff status [--exp <id>]\nmegaplan bakeoff tail --exp <id> [--profile X]\nmegaplan bakeoff compare --exp <id> [--judge <model>]\nmegaplan bakeoff pick --exp <id> --profile <name> [--rationale <text>]\nmegaplan bakeoff merge --exp <id>\nmegaplan bakeoff resume --exp <id>\nmegaplan bakeoff abandon --exp <id>\n```\n\n### 8. State\n\nOne top-level bake-off state at `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/bakeoff.json`:\n- `experiment_id`, `base_sha`, `idea_hash`, `idea_path`\n- `profiles[].{name, worktree, plan_id, pid, launched_at, outcome}`\n- `chosen_profile`, `merged_at`, `phase` (running / compared / picked / merged / abandoned)\n\nThis is a thin coordination layer. The per-profile `state.json` inside each worktree remains the authoritative per-run record.\n\n### 9. Out of scope\n\n- LLM-judge prompt-calibration fixtures and human-calibration set (Sprint 3 or later).\n- Isolated-phase replay (enabled by Sprint 1 canonical hashes, but separate UX).\n- Automatic judge scheduling post-run (keep `bakeoff compare` explicit for v1 \u2014 auto-running doubles cost for rarely-queried data).\n\n## Success criteria\n\n1. `megaplan bakeoff run --idea-file foo.md --profiles standard all-open all-kimi` launches three concurrent autos, each in its own worktree, with independent per-profile logs.\n2. `bakeoff status` shows an accurate live table while runs are in flight.\n3. After all profiles reach terminal state, `bakeoff compare` produces a `comparison.json` conforming to the documented schema \u2014 with receipts-based metrics for every completed profile and graceful nulls for failed profiles.\n4. `bakeoff pick` records a human selection; `bakeoff merge` applies exactly the chosen profile's diff to the main tree and copies all profiles' audit data into `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/`.\n5. Crashing one profile mid-run does not prevent the others from completing or the comparison from running.\n6. A resumed bake-off (`bakeoff resume`) does not re-run already-completed profiles.\n7. Worktrees are fully removed after a successful merge; retained (with crash marker) after a crash.",

      {
        "criterion": "`megaplan auto --outcome-file PATH` writes the final `DriverOutcome` JSON to PATH atomically in addition to stdout, with identical content. Existing `auto` callers that omit the flag are unaffected.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "`megaplan bakeoff run --idea-file <path> --profiles standard all-open all-kimi` launches three concurrent auto subprocesses, each with cwd set to a worktree under `<repo-parent>/.megaplan-worktrees/<exp-id>/<profile>/`, each with its own `auto.log` (stdout+stderr) and `outcome.json` (structured DriverOutcome).",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_shell",
          "read_files"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Worktrees are created with `git worktree add --detach` from a single `base_sha` captured once at bake-off start; `git branch` in the main tree shows no new branches after the run.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_shell"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "`megaplan --help` lists the `bakeoff` subcommand group (via a placeholder parser registered in `build_parser()`), and `megaplan bakeoff --help` enumerates all sub-subcommands. A smoke test modeled on `tests/test_core_without_cloud.py` enforces this.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_shell",
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "`bakeoff run --mode doc` (or `joke`/`metaplan`) fails fast with a clear error pointing at the v1 `code`-only restriction, rather than surfacing a downstream `handle_init` failure.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "`bakeoff status --exp <id>` prints an accurate table of `profile | state | phase | iter | age` while runs are in flight and after they finish, reading per-worktree `state.json` (not `outcome.json`, which may not yet exist).",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_shell",
          "read_files"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "After all profiles reach terminal state, `bakeoff compare --exp <id>` produces `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/comparison.json` with `schema_version=1` and every documented field present (nulls + `sprint1_pending: true` for scope-drift pending Sprint 1), plus a readable `comparison.md`.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_shell",
          "read_files"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "A profile that crashes mid-run is recorded with `outcome_status != \"done\"`, leaves a `BAKEOFF_CRASHED` marker in its worktree, and does not prevent sibling profiles from completing or `compare` from running.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "read_files"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "`bakeoff merge --exp <id>` uses `collect_git_diff_patch()` so the winner's patch bundles both tracked edits and untracked new files; applying it to a clean main tree creates those new files as well as updating tracked ones.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "parse_diff"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "`bakeoff pick --exp <id> --profile <name>` records `human_decision` in `comparison.json` and `bakeoff merge --exp <id>` applies exactly the chosen profile's patch via `git apply` to the main tree, with evaluation trees from all profiles copied to `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/`.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "read_files",
          "parse_diff"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "After a successful `bakeoff merge`, every worktree has been removed (`git worktree list` shows main only), only the winner's plan dir appears under `.megaplan/plans/<exp-id>-<winner>/` with `state.json.config.project_dir` rewritten to the main-tree absolute path and `archived_project_dir` preserving the old value.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_shell",
          "read_files"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Archived losing plan dirs under `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/<profile>/plan/state.json` have `config.project_dir = null` with the original absolute worktree path preserved as `config.archived_project_dir`, so no live command can cd into a removed worktree.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "read_files"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "`bakeoff resume --exp <id>` does not relaunch profiles already in AUTOMATION_TERMINAL_STATES; it only spawns auto (with `--outcome-file`) for profiles still non-terminal.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "`bakeoff merge` refuses to run when the main tree has uncommitted changes and fails cleanly (not partially) when `git apply` would conflict; `winner.patch` is left on disk for manual inspection.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "run_shell"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "`comparison.json` schema is stable \u2014 same keys emitted for failed profiles as for successful ones, using nulls plus status flags rather than omissions.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "read_files"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "The full existing test suite still passes after the feature lands.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Bake-off logs are per-profile files (never interleaved on shared stderr); `bakeoff tail` is a multiplexing convenience, not the canonical source.",
        "priority": "should",
        "requires": [
          "read_files"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "New code in `megaplan/bakeoff/` stays under ~300 lines per module and follows the existing handler/parser style used by `cloud`.",
        "priority": "should",
        "requires": [
          "read_files"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "LLM-judge cost is opt-in: omitting `--judge` never triggers a paid API call during `bakeoff compare`.",
        "priority": "should",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Real-model smoke run with two cheap profiles on a trivial idea produces a merged main tree with the winner's changes applied (including any newly created files) and no worktree leftovers.",
        "priority": "info",
        "requires": [
          "subjective_judgment",
          "run_shell"
        ]
      }

    {
      "id": "DEBT-009",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-029",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-v2"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-032",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-046",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-plan-to-extract-the-20260419-1637"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-050",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: runner inputs: the revised auto-mode snippet calls `$(cat ${idea_file})` and the chain-mode path runs `mp-chain ${chain_spec}`, but step 7's deploy flow still only does `railway link`, `railway variables --set`, and `railway up`. there is no corresponding step that copies the idea file or chain spec into `/workspace`, so a literal implementation would boot into auto/chain mode and immediately fail on missing input files.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-056",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised 'positive structural check' is still not complete enough to justify the plan text that says future direct hermes imports will fail fast by construction. step 3.4 only greps `from (run_agent|hermes_state) import ...`, and step 9.1 only bans `ast.importfrom` nodes for those modules, but the current repo already uses bare `import run_agent` in `megaplan/workers.py:1590` and `megaplan/_core/io.py:270`; a future regression that copied that import style into another execution path would bypass the stated guard without tripping the plan's validation.",

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a new critical issue is introduced on mobile. step 4.9 says to remove entrypage's mobile sticky bottom nav and 'use the same `footercontent` slot in submissioncarouselcard', but `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` renders `footercontent` inside `classname=\"hidden md:block\"`. in the current repo, the mobile sticky nav at `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:283-340` is the only mobile surface for back/next and the score toggle, so the revised plan would remove working mobile controls and replace them with a slot that is explicitly hidden on phones.",

      ]
    },

      "id": "DEBT-012",

      ]
    },
    {

      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: issue hints: the plan now wires `mode=auto|chain|idle`, but the user-requested `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec` still have no deployment path into the container. reigh's readme uploads the idea file explicitly before running `megaplan init`, and `chain.sh` exits if `/workspace/chain.yaml` is missing, so a plan that only sets these paths in `cloud.yaml` without also staging the files still leaves the requested runner modes incomplete.",

  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: if the implementation really wants `footercontent` to carry mobile actions, the plan must also include a supporting change in `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx` or render a separate mobile control surface outside the card. right now step 4.9 removes the mobile nav but does not list `submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` as a place that must change, so the required glue for mobile controls is missing.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-033",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the prior missing locations in `megaplan/workers.py` and `tests/test_workers.py` are now covered, but one supporting-infrastructure path is still absent from the steps: the repo-root generated schema copy under `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` and the tests that read it directly. without either explicitly regenerating that file or changing those tests to materialize schemas in a temp root, the plan can still leave the runtime-schema copy out of sync with the raw registry.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-035",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the plan adds _set_active_step and _clear_active_step helpers to handlers.py. _run_worker (handlers.py:135) is modified to accept a `resolved` parameter. but _run_worker is also called directly by handle_critique's sequential fallback at line 801 and 803. these calls currently don't pass `resolved`. the plan's step 2.6 for handle_critique says to set active_step after resolution at line 794 \u2014 but the _run_worker calls at 801/803 would re-resolve the agent internally (since `resolved` isn't passed). this means the agent is resolved twice on the critique sequential path. the double-resolution is wasteful but correct (same result). however, to match the plan's goal of 'resolve_agent_mode() is called once per handler invocation', the resolved tuple should be passed to these _run_worker calls too.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-megaplan-s-planning-20260409-2103"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-048",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: supporting infrastructure is still missing for mode inputs. step 3 ports `chain.yaml.example` into package templates, but step 5's `materialize_deploy_dir()` layout does not include it, step 7 deploy does not upload it or `auto.idea_file`, and the docs/validation steps do not mention any artifact-sync path before boot. that leaves the runner depending on files that the deployment plan never places on the container volume.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-058",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the location coverage is still not literally complete in the plan's validation layer because step 3.4 and step 9.1 only search for `from run_agent import` and `from hermes_state import` statements. that leaves bare `import run_agent` or `import hermes_state` outside `megaplan/agent/` unguarded even though the current repo already uses that form in availability helpers, so the 'all locations by construction' claim remains overstated.",

      "plan_ids": [

    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked the skip caller path against the real hook behavior and `removefromqueue(entryid)` looks redundant. `scoring.skipentry(entryid)` optimistically adds the id to `skippedentryids` in `src/hooks/usescoring.ts:245-249`, and `usecarouselqueue` already reactively filters skipped entries from `submissions[]` in `src/hooks/usecarouselqueue.ts:154-165`. calling `removefromqueue()` immediately afterward is safe, but it duplicates an existing state transition and should be justified as an explicit ux optimization rather than required logic.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-015",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the direct ui sign-in caller is still important here. `scorepanel` invokes its own `onrequiresignin` prop when an unauthenticated user interacts with the score ui (`src/components/scorepanel.tsx:499-515`), which is independent of the scoring hook's internal `requireauth()` path. the plan should be read as requiring that caller to dispatch `open_sign_in_modal`; otherwise one caller remains broken even if `submitscore()` is correctly wired.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-034",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i rechecked the caller paths after the revision. the raw-schema caller path through `validate_payload()` is now accounted for, but the runtime-schema caller path still is not: `strict_schema()` only reaches the on-disk runtime schemas through `ensure_runtime_layout()`, while current tests like `tests/test_schemas.py` and `tests/test_parallel_review.py` consume the already-written repo copy directly. the plan does not yet say how that caller path gets refreshed or asserted after the code change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the mobile problem is broader than entrypage. `submissionscarouselpage` already has a pre-existing mobile gap because its score panel is desktop-only and its footer actions are also hidden on mobile (`src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347-354` plus `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`). the revised plan says entrypage should 'match the carouselpage pattern', which would spread that existing gap into the one page that currently has working mobile controls.",

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",

          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

        Idea:
# Sprint 2 — Multi-profile bake-off (concurrent, worktree-isolated, audit-merged)

## Goal

Add a `megaplan bakeoff` subcommand that runs the same idea through N profiles **concurrently**, each inside its own git worktree, then a new comparison/selection step chooses one to keep. Final merge is **asymmetric**: code changes from one chosen profile, evaluation data from all profiles.

**Prerequisite**: Sprint 1 (step receipts + canonical prompt hash + scope-drift first-class metric + global audit log). This sprint depends on receipts being in place — both for the comparison step's inputs and for the audit records that get merged back.

## Why

Single-profile runs give us one data point. To answer "which profile does this kind of task best" and "which model is scope-disciplined at execute" we need side-by-side runs on identical inputs. Worktrees make the runs truly independent (no shared git state, no crossed-stream executor output). Concurrency makes the wall time bearable.

## Scope

### 1. Worktree lifecycle

- Worktrees live **sibling to the repo**, not inside `.megaplan/`. Path: `<repo-parent>/.megaplan-worktrees/<exp-id>/<profile>/`. This avoids the circular-recursion risk where megaplan's plan-dir discovery (`rglob(".megaplan")`) would otherwise walk into the worktrees.
- Each worktree is created with `git worktree add --detach <path> <base-sha>`. `base-sha` is captured once at bake-off start; every profile demonstrably starts from the same commit.
- **Detached HEADs** are deliberate — we don't want N profile branches polluting `git branch`.
- Cleanup is **explicit and deferred**: on successful completion, artifacts are copied back first, then `git worktree remove --force`. On crash, worktrees are left in place with a `BAKEOFF_CRASHED` marker file so forensic diffs survive. `megaplan bakeoff abandon <exp-id>` offers explicit cleanup later.

### 2. Concurrent execution

- The orchestrator launches N `megaplan auto` subprocesses (one per worktree, `cwd=<worktree>`) with per-profile logs redirected to `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/<profile>/auto.log`. **Do not interleave to a shared stderr** — that's unreadable at N≥3 and destroys post-mortem value.
- Use `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec` (not threads — signal handling is cleaner with subprocesses, and auto-driver already shells out per phase).
- Observability:
  - `megaplan bakeoff status [--exp <id>]` — compact table `profile | state | phase | iter | age | cost`.
  - `megaplan bakeoff tail --exp <id> [--profile X]` — either tail one log or multiplex all with `[profile]` prefixes. Unified tail is a convenience; per-profile log files remain canonical.

### 3. Comparison / selection step

A **standalone subcommand**, not an auto-integrated phase. Invoked separately so it can be re-run with a different judge, resumed, or retried after the bake-off completes.

`megaplan bakeoff compare --exp <id> [--judge <model>]`

**Inputs** per profile:
- `state.json` (final state, iteration counts, rework cycles, escalations)
- All step receipts (from Sprint 1) — the primary source of structured metrics
- Phase artifacts (`plan_v1.md`, `critique_v*.json`, `execution.json`, `review_output.json`)
- `git diff <base-sha>..HEAD` from the worktree
- Auto-driver's final `DriverOutcome` JSON

**Decision tier** — three layers, advisory-then-authoritative:
1. **Auto-computed metrics** — always produced. Diff stats, test pass/fail, rework cycle count, escalations, review outcome, duration, tokens, scope-drift severity per phase.
2. **LLM judge** — configurable model (default: a model *not* present as an executor in any profile being compared — e.g., if the profiles use kimi and glm, judge with claude or gpt-5). Pairwise structured prompt: "Given these two bundles of artifacts, rank them and flag any scope drift, quality concerns, or missed requirements." Advisory only.
3. **Human pick** — `megaplan bakeoff pick --exp <id> --profile <name> [--rationale ...]` records the final selection. If the human agrees with the judge, one command.

**Output**: `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/comparison.json` + a readable `comparison.md`.

### 4. Comparison schema (load-bearing)

This schema is the thing that accumulates across runs into a dataset. Version it explicitly (`schema_version: 1`). Write the same fields even when a profile failed early (use nulls + flags, never missing keys).

Minimum fields:
- `experiment_id`, `base_sha`, `idea_hash` (canonical hash of the idea input)
- `profiles[]`:
  - `name`, `worktree_path`, `plan_id`, `outcome_status` (done / stalled / failed / escalated / aborted)
  - `metrics`: `duration_s`, `cost_usd`, `rework_cycles`, `escalations`, `review_verdict`, `diff_lines`, `tests_added`, `scope_drift_severity_by_phase`
  - `receipts_ref` — relative path list to the receipts that belong to this profile
- `judge_verdict`: `{ judge_model, rank[], rationale_per_profile, scope_drift_flags, concerns[] }`
- `human_decision`: `{ chosen_profile, rationale, decided_at }` (populated by `bakeoff pick`)

### 5. Merge mechanics (asymmetric)

`megaplan bakeoff merge --exp <id>` applies:

- **Code half** (from one chosen worktree): `git diff <base-sha>..<chosen-worktree-HEAD>` piped to `git apply` in the main tree. Not `git merge` — we don't want throwaway-branch commit history. `git apply` fails loudly on conflicts with any main-tree work done in parallel, which is the right behavior.
- **Evaluation half** (from *all* worktrees): copy `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/<profile>/` trees back. Paths are profile-namespaced by construction — no collision.
- **Chosen plan dir**: the winning profile's `.megaplan/plans/<plan-id>/` is copied into the main tree's `.megaplan/plans/` so follow-up work can reference it. Losing profiles' plan dirs remain only in the bake-off archive, not in the live plan list.

Gotcha to solve explicitly: `state.json.config.project_dir` contains absolute paths from the worktree. The merge step either rewrites those paths to the main-tree path or marks archived plan dirs as read-only forensic data that must not be loaded by live commands.

### 6. Failure & partial success

- Each profile exits with a `DriverOutcome.status` in `{done, stalled, escalated, failed, aborted, cap, blocked, awaiting_human, tiebreaker_*}`.
- The bake-off treats anything other than `done` as a non-winner candidate but **still surfaces it to compare** — a profile stalling at gate is valuable evaluation data ("this profile can't plan this kind of idea").
- `megaplan bakeoff resume <exp-id>` checks each profile's state, relaunches auto only for profiles that haven't reached terminal, leaves winners alone.

### 7. CLI surface

```
megaplan bakeoff run --idea-file <path> --profiles A B C [--mode code] [--exp-id <name>]
megaplan bakeoff status [--exp <id>]
megaplan bakeoff tail --exp <id> [--profile X]
megaplan bakeoff compare --exp <id> [--judge <model>]
megaplan bakeoff pick --exp <id> --profile <name> [--rationale <text>]
megaplan bakeoff merge --exp <id>
megaplan bakeoff resume --exp <id>
megaplan bakeoff abandon --exp <id>
```

### 8. State

One top-level bake-off state at `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/bakeoff.json`:
- `experiment_id`, `base_sha`, `idea_hash`, `idea_path`
- `profiles[].{name, worktree, plan_id, pid, launched_at, outcome}`
- `chosen_profile`, `merged_at`, `phase` (running / compared / picked / merged / abandoned)

This is a thin coordination layer. The per-profile `state.json` inside each worktree remains the authoritative per-run record.

### 9. Out of scope

- LLM-judge prompt-calibration fixtures and human-calibration set (Sprint 3 or later).
- Isolated-phase replay (enabled by Sprint 1 canonical hashes, but separate UX).
- Automatic judge scheduling post-run (keep `bakeoff compare` explicit for v1 — auto-running doubles cost for rarely-queried data).

## Success criteria

1. `megaplan bakeoff run --idea-file foo.md --profiles standard all-open all-kimi` launches three concurrent autos, each in its own worktree, with independent per-profile logs.
2. `bakeoff status` shows an accurate live table while runs are in flight.
3. After all profiles reach terminal state, `bakeoff compare` produces a `comparison.json` conforming to the documented schema — with receipts-based metrics for every completed profile and graceful nulls for failed profiles.
4. `bakeoff pick` records a human selection; `bakeoff merge` applies exactly the chosen profile's diff to the main tree and copies all profiles' audit data into `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/`.
5. Crashing one profile mid-run does not prevent the others from completing or the comparison from running.
6. A resumed bake-off (`bakeoff resume`) does not re-run already-completed profiles.
7. Worktrees are fully removed after a successful merge; retained (with crash marker) after a crash.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Multi-profile Bake-off (`megaplan bakeoff`) — Revision 3

## Overview

Add a `bakeoff` command group that runs the same idea through N named profiles concurrently in sibling git worktrees, then compares, picks, and asymmetrically merges results back. Each profile runs a full `megaplan auto` loop in isolation.

**Repository shape (confirmed by exploration)**:
- CLI dispatch: top-level `build_parser` at `megaplan/cli.py:864`; `main()` routes `cloud` / `auto` / `chain` / `tiebreaker` as first-class branches before falling through to `COMMAND_HANDLERS` (`cli.py:1193-1272`). The `cloud` branch (`cli.py:1196-1207`) is the model for a first-class subgroup with its own nested parser.
- Auto driver: `megaplan/auto.py` — `build_auto_parser` (line 484), `run_auto(root, args)` (line 561), `drive(plan, *, cwd=..., writer=sys.stdout.write)` (line 177; note the pluggable `writer`), `_run_megaplan` shell-outs (line 77). `DriverOutcome` at lines 50-74 serialises to JSON. Exit-code mapping: done=0, aborted=0, stalled=2, escalated=3, cap=4, blocked=5, other=1.
- `drive()` writes **progress** to its `writer` param and `run_auto()` writes the **final DriverOutcome JSON** to `sys.stdout` (auto.py:574). Progress and outcome share a single stdout today. For bake-off we need them on separate channels so we can tee progress to a log file while parsing the outcome cleanly.
- Existing git-diff helper with untracked-file support: `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` in `megaplan/_core/io.py:358-415` — runs `git diff --binary --no-ext-diff HEAD` plus `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard` plus `git diff --no-index /dev/null <file>` for each untracked path. Regression-covered by `tests/test_io_git_patch.py`. **Bake-off merge must use this helper.**
- State: `PlanState` TypedDict at `megaplan/types.py:134-148`; `load_plan` / `save_state` at `megaplan/_core/state.py:95-98`, 218. `config.project_dir` (types.py:37) is an absolute string — path-rewrite gotcha on merge.
- Profiles: TOML-based loader at `megaplan/profiles/__init__.py:119-170`. `--profile <name>` expands into `--phase-model` args. Built-in + user layer; project layer requires the toml being visible in the worktree checkout.
- Plan artifacts under `.megaplan/plans/<plan-id>/`: `plan_v{N}.md`, `critique_v{N}.json`, `gate.json`, `finalize.json`, `execution.json` (+ `execution_batch_{N}.json`), `review.json`, `faults.json`. These, plus `state.json` and the full patch from `collect_git_diff_patch`, are the comparison inputs.
- `tests/test_core_without_cloud.py` is the precedent for a CLI help/import smoke test on first-class subgroups — we'll add an analogous `test_core_without_bakeoff.py`.
- No existing worktree code, no asyncio orchestration, no step-receipts infrastructure.

**Key constraints**:
1. Worktrees must live outside the repo tree (`rglob(".megaplan")` recursion risk, state.py:70, cli.py:410) — use `<repo-parent>/.megaplan-worktrees/<exp-id>/<profile>/`.
2. Each phase already runs in its own subprocess, so concurrent profile runs are just parallel outer subprocesses. No internal refactor to `auto.py`'s core loop.
3. **Sprint 1 divergence (accepted v1 decision)**: the brief lists Sprint 1 (receipts / canonical prompt hash / first-class scope-drift metric / global audit log) as a prerequisite. None are in the tree. Sprint 2 ships independently with `scope_drift_severity_by_phase` as nulls + `"sprint1_pending": true`, and `receipts_ref` derived from `HistoryEntry.output_file` (`types.py:105`). `schema_version=1` is forward-compatible: Sprint 1's richer receipts become additive fields without a schema bump. This matches Peter's documented "stubs over omissions" principle — the comparison dataset accumulates with stable keys, and a later sprint backfills the null fields. Not a pending question; a settled v1 scope decision.
4. Profile discovery from inside a worktree: Sprint 2 supports built-in + user-layer profiles only; project-layer `.megaplan/profiles.toml` is out of scope unless it's a git-tracked file.
5. `bakeoff run` is v1-restricted to `--mode code`. `--mode doc|joke|metaplan` requires `--output` and per-mode validation (`handle_init`), which adds surface area without demand. Explicit fail-fast if a non-code mode is requested.
6. Bake-off always uses subprocess `cwd=<worktree>` for isolation; we do not plumb `--work-dir` through bake-off (it does not affect `drive()`, `auto.py:563-573`).
7. **Judge contract (single unambiguous rule)**:
   - `bakeoff compare` **without `--judge`** → no judge runs; `judge_verdict = null`. No paid API calls.
   - `bakeoff compare --judge auto` → dynamic selection: pick the first of `claude`, `codex`, `gpt-5` NOT referenced as `execute` or `plan` in any compared profile; if none free, error out pointing the user at `--judge <model>`.
   - `bakeoff compare --judge <model>` → use that model explicitly.
   The flag value is the single source of truth — there is no implicit default that fires paid inference.

## Phase 0: Auto-driver enabling change (prerequisite for Phase 2)

### Step 1: Add `--outcome-file` to `megaplan auto` (`megaplan/auto.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Extend** `build_auto_parser` (`megaplan/auto.py:484-558`) with `--outcome-file PATH` — optional absolute path where `run_auto` writes the final `DriverOutcome` JSON in addition to stdout.
2. **Update** `run_auto(root, args)` (`megaplan/auto.py:561-590`) — after computing `outcome`, if `args.outcome_file` is set, write `outcome.to_json()` to that path atomically (tempfile + rename) before the existing `sys.stdout.write`. No change to return code, no change to stdout contract.
3. **Add** one unit test in `tests/test_auto.py` asserting the outcome JSON is written to the path when the flag is passed, identical content to stdout.
4. **Rationale (addresses FLAG-002 / correctness-2)**: bake-off redirects subprocess stdout+stderr to `auto.log` for human-readable forensics and reads the structured outcome from `outcome-file`. No fragile log-tail parsing, no in-process `drive()`, no stdout format change.

## Phase 1: Foundation — Module, CLI Wiring, State

### Step 2: Scaffold `megaplan/bakeoff/` and register the subcommand group (`megaplan/bakeoff/__init__.py`, `megaplan/bakeoff/cli.py`, `megaplan/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `megaplan/bakeoff/__init__.py` (empty) and `megaplan/bakeoff/cli.py` with `_register_bakeoff_subcommands(parser)` and `run_bakeoff_cli(root, args)` — mirror the `cloud` group's pattern (`megaplan/cli.py:1196-1207`). Sub-subcommands: `run`, `status`, `tail`, `compare`, `pick`, `merge`, `resume`, `abandon`.
2. **Add** a first-class `bakeoff` branch in `main()` (`megaplan/cli.py:1196-1207`) immediately after the `cloud` branch, using the same pre-parser dispatch pattern.
3. **Register a placeholder `bakeoff` parser inside `build_parser()`** so `megaplan --help` surfaces the group. Follow `cloud`'s exact approach: a no-op `subparsers.add_parser("bakeoff", help="...", add_help=False)` whose help text points users at `megaplan bakeoff --help`. The pre-parser branch in `main()` takes precedence at runtime; the placeholder exists for discovery only.
4. **Argparse shape** per the brief and the judge contract in Overview constraint #7:
   - `run` — `--idea-file` (required), `--profiles A B C` (nargs="+", required), `--mode` (choices=["code"], default="code"), `--exp-id`, `--detach`, `--allow-dirty`. (No `--judge` on `run` — judging is explicit at `compare` time only.)
   - `status` — `--exp`.
   - `tail` — `--exp`, `--profile`.
   - `compare` — `--exp`, `--judge MODEL_OR_AUTO` (default `None`; accepts `auto` or any model name), `--force`.
   - `pick` — `--exp`, `--profile`, `--rationale`.
   - `merge` / `resume` / `abandon` — `--exp`.
5. **Mode guard**: `bakeoff run` rejects any `--mode` other than `code` with `CliError("bakeoff_unsupported_mode", "bake-off v1 supports --mode code only; doc/joke/metaplan require --output pass-through, deferred to a follow-up.")`. Prevents silent `handle_init` failures downstream.

### Step 3: Bake-off state schema + helpers (`megaplan/bakeoff/state.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Introduce** TypedDicts (style after `megaplan/types.py:134`):
   - `BakeoffProfileRecord` — `name`, `worktree`, `plan_id`, `pid`, `launched_at`, `terminated_at`, `outcome` (DriverOutcome dict or null), `log_path`, `outcome_path`.
   - `BakeoffState` — `schema_version: int = 1`, `experiment_id`, `base_sha`, `idea_hash` (sha256 of idea-file bytes), `idea_path`, `mode`, `profiles: list[BakeoffProfileRecord]`, `phase: Literal["running", "compared", "picked", "merged", "abandoned"]`, `chosen_profile`, `merged_at`, `judge_model`.
2. **Add** `load_bakeoff_state(root, exp_id)` / `save_bakeoff_state(root, state)` writing to `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/bakeoff.json`. Atomic write (tempfile + rename) — mirror `_core/state.py:save_state`.
3. **Add** `bakeoff_root(root, exp_id)` returning `<root>/.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/`, `worktree_root(root, exp_id)` returning `<root>.parent / ".megaplan-worktrees" / exp_id`. Never put worktrees under `root`.
4. **Add** `hash_idea_file(path) -> str` (sha256 hex of UTF-8 bytes) for `idea_hash`.

## Phase 2: Worktree Lifecycle + Concurrent Execution

### Step 4: Worktree provisioning (`megaplan/bakeoff/worktree.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `create_worktree(repo, target, base_sha)` via `subprocess.run(["git", "worktree", "add", "--detach", str(target), base_sha], cwd=repo, check=True)`. Raise `CliError("bakeoff_worktree_failed", ...)` on non-zero with stderr captured.
2. **Implement** `capture_base_sha(repo)` via `git rev-parse HEAD` — called exactly once at bake-off start.
3. **Implement** `remove_worktree(target, force=True)` via `git worktree remove --force <target>`; on success clean up the empty parent `<repo-parent>/.megaplan-worktrees/<exp-id>/` if empty.
4. **Implement** `mark_crashed(target, reason)` writing `BAKEOFF_CRASHED` JSON `{reason, ts, pid}` into the worktree root.
5. **Dirty-tree guard**: refuse to create worktrees if `git status --porcelain` in the main repo is non-empty AND `--allow-dirty` is not passed. Terse error pointing at `git status`.

### Step 5: Async orchestrator (`megaplan/bakeoff/orchestrator.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Implement** `async def run_bakeoff(root, idea_path, profiles, mode, exp_id, ...) -> BakeoffState`. Flow:
   1. Resolve `idea_path` to absolute; compute `idea_hash`. Capture `base_sha`. Create `bakeoff_root`; write initial `bakeoff.json` with `phase="running"`.
   2. Per profile (serial): create worktree at `worktree_root/<profile>/`; run `megaplan init --project-dir <worktree> --name <exp-id> --idea-file <abs-idea-path> --profile <name> --mode code` via `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec` with stdout/stderr to `bakeoff_root/<profile>/init.log`. Abort the whole bake-off if any init fails — worktrees already created are left with a `BAKEOFF_CRASHED` marker.
   3. Spawn N concurrent `python -m megaplan auto --plan <exp-id> --outcome-file <bakeoff_root>/<profile>/outcome.json` subprocesses via `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec` with `cwd=<worktree>`, `stdout` and `stderr` both redirected to file handles opened on `bakeoff_root/<profile>/auto.log`. Record `pid`, `launched_at`, `log_path`, `outcome_path` in state.
   4. `asyncio.gather(*wait_tasks, return_exceptions=True)` — one profile crashing MUST NOT cancel siblings. For a task that raises or a subprocess exits with no `outcome.json`, call `mark_crashed(<worktree>, <reason>)` and synthesize a `DriverOutcome(status="failed", reason=<stderr tail>, ...)` in the record.
   5. For each exited subprocess with an `outcome.json`, parse it as the `DriverOutcome` and persist into the profile record. This cleanly separates structured outcome (outcome.json) from human log (auto.log).
   6. Do not auto-advance `phase`; it stays `"running"` until `compare` runs.
2. **Implement** `run_bakeoff_run_handler(root, args)` that invokes `asyncio.run(run_bakeoff(...))` and, unless `--detach` is passed, streams a terse status table to stdout every 5 s (profile | state | phase | iter | age) built by polling each `load_plan(<worktree>, <exp-id>)`.
3. **Plan name inside worktree**: use `<exp-id>` verbatim so downstream bake-off commands don't have to resolve per-profile plan names.
4. **Live status polling does not read `outcome.json`** (it isn't written until subprocess exit). Use `state.json` for in-flight state and `outcome.json` only after the subprocess has exited.

### Step 6: `bakeoff status` and `bakeoff tail` (`megaplan/bakeoff/handlers.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **`status`**: load `bakeoff.json`; for each profile with a worktree still present, `load_plan(<worktree>, <exp-id>)` to read `current_state`, `iteration`, `meta.total_cost_usd`; compute age from `launched_at`. Render a compact table (prefer `rich` if available, plain text otherwise). If no `--exp`, list the most-recent bake-off.
2. **`tail`**: with `--profile X`, exec `tail -F <log>`. Without, fan in one `tail -F` subprocess per log, prefix each line with `[profile] `, write to shared stdout. Per-profile log files are canonical; multiplex is convenience only.

## Phase 3: Comparison + Selection + Merge

### Step 7: Structural metrics collection (`megaplan/bakeoff/metrics.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `collect_profile_metrics(bakeoff_state, profile_record) -> dict` pulling from artifacts, null-safe (per-key nulls, never missing keys):
   - `duration_s` — from `launched_at` → `terminated_at`.
   - `cost_usd` — sum `state["history"][i]["cost_usd"]` (`types.py:100`) with `meta.total_cost_usd` (`types.py:57`) as fallback if present.
   - `rework_cycles` — `max(0, count(history[step=="review"]) - 1)`; corroborate with any `review_rework_cycle_count` event on `DriverOutcome.events` (`auto.py:60`).
   - `escalations` — count `history` entries with `recommendation == "ESCALATE"` plus `last_gate.recommendation == "ESCALATE"` if unrecorded elsewhere.
   - `review_verdict` — parse `review.json` final `verdict`.
   - `diff_lines` — derived from the **full** patch produced by `collect_git_diff_patch(<worktree>)` (lines starting with `+` / `-`, excluding hunk headers). Falls back to `null` if the worktree is gone pre-merge.
   - `tests_added` — from the same full patch, count new files under `tests/` or matching `test_*.py` that are pure additions.
   - `scope_drift_severity_by_phase` — `{"plan": null, "critique": null, "gate": null, "finalize": null, "execute": null, "review": null}` plus `"sprint1_pending": true`. Upgraded when Sprint 1 receipts land; no schema bump.
   - `final_state`, `outcome_status` — from the persisted `DriverOutcome`.
2. **`receipts_ref`**: list `state["history"][i]["output_file"]` paths relative to `<worktree>/.megaplan/plans/<exp-id>/`. If `.megaplan/plans/<exp-id>/receipts/*.json` exists (Sprint 1), include those too.

### Step 8: Optional LLM judge (`megaplan/bakeoff/judge.py`) — implements the Overview constraint #7 contract
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Single entry rule** (no implicit default, addresses the `correctness` flag): `run_judge` is called **only if `args.judge` is set**. `bakeoff compare` with no `--judge` records `judge_verdict = null` and `bakeoff_state.judge_model = null`; no paid API call is ever dispatched.
2. **Resolve** the judge model:
   - `args.judge == "auto"` → scan the compared profiles' effective phase maps (the per-profile agent assignments resolved from `--profile` via `megaplan/profiles/__init__.py:143-170`); pick the first of `["claude", "codex", "gpt-5"]` that does NOT appear as `execute` or `plan` for any compared profile. If none free, raise `CliError("bakeoff_no_free_judge", "no auto-selectable judge; pass --judge <model> explicitly.")`.
   - `args.judge` otherwise → use that model name verbatim (no free-slot check).
3. **Implement** `async def run_judge(bakeoff_state, metrics_by_profile, judge_model) -> JudgeVerdict` bundling per-profile artifacts + metrics + full-patch summary into a single prompt. Output JSON: `{judge_model, rank[], rationale_per_profile, scope_drift_flags, concerns[]}`. Uses an agent client from `megaplan/agent/` (e.g. Claude via `anthropic` SDK).
4. **Persist** `judge_verdict` into `comparison.json` and `bakeoff_state.judge_model` into `bakeoff.json`. If a user re-runs `bakeoff compare --judge <different>` with `--force`, `judge_model` is overwritten.

### Step 9: `bakeoff compare` (`megaplan/bakeoff/handlers.py`, `megaplan/bakeoff/comparison.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `handle_compare(root, args)`:
   1. Load `bakeoff.json`; refuse if `phase == "abandoned"`.
   2. For each profile, call `collect_profile_metrics` — emit full schema with nulls for failed/early-exit profiles, never drop.
   3. If `args.judge` is set, call `run_judge` per Step 8's rules; otherwise `judge_verdict = null`.
   4. Write `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/comparison.json` per the brief's schema: `schema_version=1`, `experiment_id`, `base_sha`, `idea_hash`, `profiles[]`, `judge_verdict`, `human_decision=null`. Alphabetised keys.
   5. Render `comparison.md` — short, readable metrics table + judge rationale (or "judge skipped: no `--judge` flag" line if null).
   6. Set `bakeoff.phase = "compared"`, save.
2. **Re-runnable**: subsequent `compare` is a no-op warning unless `--force` is passed. `--force` re-runs metrics and judge (if requested), overwriting `comparison.json` and `bakeoff.judge_model`.

### Step 10: `bakeoff pick` (`megaplan/bakeoff/handlers.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Implement** `handle_pick(root, args)`:
   1. Load state + `comparison.json`; verify `args.profile` exists. Warn (don't block) if chosen profile's `outcome_status != "done"`.
   2. Update `comparison.json.human_decision = {chosen_profile, rationale, decided_at}`.
   3. Update `bakeoff.chosen_profile`, `bakeoff.phase = "picked"`; save.

### Step 11: Asymmetric merge (`megaplan/bakeoff/merge.py`, `megaplan/bakeoff/handlers.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Preflight**: `bakeoff.phase` must be `"picked"`; main tree must be clean (`git status --porcelain`). Fail precisely otherwise.
2. **Code half (winner only)**:
   1. In the winner worktree, capture the patch via `collect_git_diff_patch(<winner-worktree>)` (`megaplan/_core/io.py:358`) — this bundles tracked `git diff --binary HEAD` with `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard` expanded to `git diff --no-index /dev/null <file>` patches. Persist to `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/winner.patch`.
   2. `git apply --check` against the main tree; on success, `git apply`. On conflict, leave `winner.patch` on disk and fail loudly — no 3-way merge, no rollback of main.
   3. Refuse to proceed if `collect_git_diff_patch` returns the sentinel `"No git changes detected."` — that would mean the winner has no diff, which is a no-op merge the user should confirm.
3. **Evaluation half (all profiles)**:
   1. For each profile, copy `<worktree>/.megaplan/plans/<exp-id>/` → `<root>/.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/<profile>/plan/`.
   2. **Rewrite archived `state.json.config.project_dir`**: before writing, set `config.project_dir = None` and store the original string in `config.archived_project_dir = <old absolute path>`. Ensures workers never try to cd into a removed worktree.
   3. Copy per-profile `auto.log`, `init.log`, `outcome.json`, and the profile's outcome stanza already in `bakeoff.json`.
4. **Chosen plan dir (winner only)**:
   1. Copy `<winner-worktree>/.megaplan/plans/<exp-id>/` → `<root>/.megaplan/plans/<exp-id>-<winner-profile>/` so it does not collide with any concurrent main-tree plan.
   2. Rewrite its `state.json.config.project_dir` to `str(root)`; preserve old path as `config.archived_project_dir`.
   3. Losing plan dirs remain only under `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/<profile>/plan/` (not under `.megaplan/plans/`), so `megaplan list` tree-search (`cli.py:410`, which globs `.megaplan/plans/*`) will not surface them.
5. **Cleanup**: `git worktree remove --force` each worktree after all copies succeed. Report but don't roll back if a remove fails — the data is archived.
6. **State**: set `bakeoff.phase = "merged"`, `bakeoff.merged_at`, save.

### Step 12: `bakeoff resume` and `bakeoff abandon` (`megaplan/bakeoff/handlers.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **`resume`**: load state; for each profile where the worktree exists AND `load_plan(<worktree>, <exp-id>)["current_state"] not in AUTOMATION_TERMINAL_STATES` (`types.py:25`), relaunch auto with `--outcome-file <bakeoff_root>/<profile>/outcome.json`. Winners untouched; existing worktrees and init artifacts reused.
2. **`abandon`**: load state; for each profile, `remove_worktree` (tolerate missing / crash-markers); `phase = "abandoned"`. `<root>/.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/` is retained for audit.

## Phase 4: Tests + Docs

### Step 13: Unit tests (`tests/bakeoff/test_state.py`, `tests/bakeoff/test_worktree.py`, `tests/bakeoff/test_metrics.py`, `tests/bakeoff/test_comparison.py`, `tests/bakeoff/test_merge.py`, `tests/bakeoff/test_judge.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **State** — round-trip `BakeoffState`, atomic write, `schema_version=1` pinned.
2. **Worktree** — temp git-repo fixture; `create_worktree` produces a detached-HEAD checkout outside the repo tree, `remove_worktree` cleans up, `mark_crashed` writes the marker.
3. **Metrics** — synthetic plan dir with fabricated `state.json`/`review.json`; all null-safe paths still produce full schema. One case uses an untracked new file and asserts it's counted in `diff_lines`/`tests_added`.
4. **Comparison** — `comparison.json` is schema-stable for mixed done/failed profiles; `receipts_ref` paths are relative.
5. **Merge** — synthetic two-profile bake-off:
   - Winner's patch (produced by `collect_git_diff_patch` with both edits and **untracked files**) applies cleanly to a clean main tree — asserts untracked files land.
   - `git apply` rejects on dirty main tree.
   - Loser's plan is NOT registered under `.megaplan/plans/` and its archived `state.json.config.project_dir` is `None` with `archived_project_dir` retaining the old value.
   - Winner's copied plan has `project_dir` rewritten to the main tree.
6. **Judge contract** (addresses `correctness` flag) — three unit tests on `handle_compare`:
   - `--judge` omitted → `run_judge` is never called (assert via mock) and `comparison.json.judge_verdict is None`.
   - `--judge auto` with two profiles whose execute/plan map covers `claude` and `codex` → `codex` or a third-tier model is selected per the ordered list; if all three are in use, `CliError("bakeoff_no_free_judge", ...)` is raised.
   - `--judge gpt-5` → `run_judge` is invoked with `judge_model="gpt-5"` regardless of phase-map content.

### Step 14: CLI help / import smoke test (`tests/test_core_without_bakeoff.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Mirror** `tests/test_core_without_cloud.py` for the bake-off subgroup: assert `megaplan --help` lists `bakeoff`, that `megaplan bakeoff --help` works, and that core commands (`status`, `list`) still function when optional bake-off deps (e.g. a missing judge-model SDK) are absent.

### Step 15: Integration test with fake auto (`tests/bakeoff/test_integration.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Stub** by monkey-patching `megaplan.bakeoff.orchestrator._spawn_auto` (a thin internal wrapper around `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec`) to a fake coroutine that writes a canned `state.json`, `review.json`, **`outcome.json`**, and a minimal `auto.log` line before exiting with the scripted returncode.
2. **Happy path**: `bakeoff run` spawns three fakes concurrently, status table populates, `compare` emits a schema-valid `comparison.json` with `judge_verdict = null` (no `--judge` passed), `pick` + `merge` complete, worktrees are gone, `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/` contains all three profiles' audit trees, `.megaplan/plans/` contains only the winner's namespaced copy.
3. **Crash isolation**: one fake raises; the other two complete; `compare` runs; crashed worktree has `BAKEOFF_CRASHED`; its `outcome_status` in `comparison.json` is `"failed"` with nulls elsewhere.
4. **Resume**: one profile marked non-terminal; `bakeoff resume` relaunches only that profile.
5. **Untracked-file merge**: the winner fake writes a new file under `src/`; assert `winner.patch` contains the `--no-index` untracked-file stanza and `git apply` adds it to the main tree.

### Step 16: Surface the command + prerequisite note (`README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a README section documenting `bakeoff run|status|tail|compare|pick|merge|resume|abandon` with one example, and the judge contract (omit `--judge` → skip; `--judge auto` → pick a free model; `--judge <model>` → explicit).
2. **CHANGELOG** entry flagging this is Sprint 2 with the Sprint 1 caveat: `scope_drift_severity_by_phase` fields emit nulls + `sprint1_pending: true` until receipts/canonical-hash/scope-drift land. Schema v1 is forward-compatible; new receipt-derived fields arrive additively.

## Execution Order

1. **Phase 0** (`--outcome-file`) lands first — a tiny surface-area change that unblocks clean outcome parsing for bake-off orchestration.
2. **Phase 1** (scaffold + CLI wiring including the `build_parser` placeholder + state schema) — everything else depends on this.
3. **Phase 2** (worktree + async orchestrator + status/tail) — end-to-end runnable without compare.
4. **Phase 3** (compare → pick → merge, with `collect_git_diff_patch` in merge and the judge contract from Overview #7).
5. **Phase 4** (tests + docs) lands alongside each phase where possible; integration test at the end.

## Validation Order

1. Phase 0 unit test for `--outcome-file` (cheap, decouples the channel split from later work).
2. Unit tests per bake-off module as they're written; the judge-contract unit tests land with Step 8.
3. CLI help smoke test (`test_core_without_bakeoff.py`) — catches the `build_parser` placeholder omission early.
4. Integration test with stubbed agents — exercises orchestration end-to-end including the untracked-file merge path and the `--judge` off/auto/explicit branches.
5. Real-agent smoke run with 2 cheap profiles on a trivial idea, on a throwaway branch, verifying worktree semantics and merge behavior on actual git state.
6. Full `pytest` suite to catch regressions in unrelated CLI paths.

This matches Peter's stubs-over-omissions principle and is treated as a settled v1 scope decision, not a standing question.",
    "Judge contract is explicit and single-rule: `bakeoff compare` with no `--judge` never fires a paid API call (judge_verdict=null); `--judge auto` triggers dynamic selection of the first of claude/codex/gpt-5 not used as execute/plan in any compared profile, erroring out if none are free; `--judge <model>` uses that model verbatim.",
    "`<exp-id>` is the plan name inside every worktree, so bake-off sub-commands don't resolve per-profile plan names.",
    "Dirty main tree blocks `bakeoff run` unless `--allow-dirty` is passed, keeping `base_sha` meaningful.",
    "`asyncio.create_subprocess_exec` is the concurrency primitive; no new heavyweight deps. Live status uses `rich` if already available, plain text otherwise.",

    "`bakeoff merge` uses `git apply` on a patch from `collect_git_diff_patch()` (not `git merge`) and fails loudly on conflicts. No 3-way merge, no rollback of main.",
    "On crash the worktree stays with a `BAKEOFF_CRASHED` marker; successful merge removes all worktrees; `bakeoff abandon` is explicit cleanup.",

    "Profile discovery in worktrees supports built-in + user-layer profiles only; project-layer `.megaplan/profiles.toml` requires a git-tracked file to be visible in the fresh checkout."

  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 3,
    "idea": "# Sprint 2 \u2014 Multi-profile bake-off (concurrent, worktree-isolated, audit-merged)\n\n## Goal\n\nAdd a `megaplan bakeoff` subcommand that runs the same idea through N profiles **concurrently**, each inside its own git worktree, then a new comparison/selection step chooses one to keep. Final merge is **asymmetric**: code changes from one chosen profile, evaluation data from all profiles.\n\n**Prerequisite**: Sprint 1 (step receipts + canonical prompt hash + scope-drift first-class metric + global audit log). This sprint depends on receipts being in place \u2014 both for the comparison step's inputs and for the audit records that get merged back.\n\n## Why\n\nSingle-profile runs give us one data point. To answer \"which profile does this kind of task best\" and \"which model is scope-disciplined at execute\" we need side-by-side runs on identical inputs. Worktrees make the runs truly independent (no shared git state, no crossed-stream executor output). Concurrency makes the wall time bearable.\n\n## Scope\n\n### 1. Worktree lifecycle\n\n- Worktrees live **sibling to the repo**, not inside `.megaplan/`. Path: `<repo-parent>/.megaplan-worktrees/<exp-id>/<profile>/`. This avoids the circular-recursion risk where megaplan's plan-dir discovery (`rglob(\".megaplan\")`) would otherwise walk into the worktrees.\n- Each worktree is created with `git worktree add --detach <path> <base-sha>`. `base-sha` is captured once at bake-off start; every profile demonstrably starts from the same commit.\n- **Detached HEADs** are deliberate \u2014 we don't want N profile branches polluting `git branch`.\n- Cleanup is **explicit and deferred**: on successful completion, artifacts are copied back first, then `git worktree remove --force`. On crash, worktrees are left in place with a `BAKEOFF_CRASHED` marker file so forensic diffs survive. `megaplan bakeoff abandon <exp-id>` offers explicit cleanup later.\n\n### 2. Concurrent execution\n\n- The orchestrator launches N `megaplan auto` subprocesses (one per worktree, `cwd=<worktree>`) with per-profile logs redirected to `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/<profile>/auto.log`. **Do not interleave to a shared stderr** \u2014 that's unreadable at N\u22653 and destroys post-mortem value.\n- Use `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec` (not threads \u2014 signal handling is cleaner with subprocesses, and auto-driver already shells out per phase).\n- Observability:\n  - `megaplan bakeoff status [--exp <id>]` \u2014 compact table `profile | state | phase | iter | age | cost`.\n  - `megaplan bakeoff tail --exp <id> [--profile X]` \u2014 either tail one log or multiplex all with `[profile]` prefixes. Unified tail is a convenience; per-profile log files remain canonical.\n\n### 3. Comparison / selection step\n\nA **standalone subcommand**, not an auto-integrated phase. Invoked separately so it can be re-run with a different judge, resumed, or retried after the bake-off completes.\n\n`megaplan bakeoff compare --exp <id> [--judge <model>]`\n\n**Inputs** per profile:\n- `state.json` (final state, iteration counts, rework cycles, escalations)\n- All step receipts (from Sprint 1) \u2014 the primary source of structured metrics\n- Phase artifacts (`plan_v1.md`, `critique_v*.json`, `execution.json`, `review_output.json`)\n- `git diff <base-sha>..HEAD` from the worktree\n- Auto-driver's final `DriverOutcome` JSON\n\n**Decision tier** \u2014 three layers, advisory-then-authoritative:\n1. **Auto-computed metrics** \u2014 always produced. Diff stats, test pass/fail, rework cycle count, escalations, review outcome, duration, tokens, scope-drift severity per phase.\n2. **LLM judge** \u2014 configurable model (default: a model *not* present as an executor in any profile being compared \u2014 e.g., if the profiles use kimi and glm, judge with claude or gpt-5). Pairwise structured prompt: \"Given these two bundles of artifacts, rank them and flag any scope drift, quality concerns, or missed requirements.\" Advisory only.\n3. **Human pick** \u2014 `megaplan bakeoff pick --exp <id> --profile <name> [--rationale ...]` records the final selection. If the human agrees with the judge, one command.\n\n**Output**: `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/comparison.json` + a readable `comparison.md`.\n\n### 4. Comparison schema (load-bearing)\n\nThis schema is the thing that accumulates across runs into a dataset. Version it explicitly (`schema_version: 1`). Write the same fields even when a profile failed early (use nulls + flags, never missing keys).\n\nMinimum fields:\n- `experiment_id`, `base_sha`, `idea_hash` (canonical hash of the idea input)\n- `profiles[]`:\n  - `name`, `worktree_path`, `plan_id`, `outcome_status` (done / stalled / failed / escalated / aborted)\n  - `metrics`: `duration_s`, `cost_usd`, `rework_cycles`, `escalations`, `review_verdict`, `diff_lines`, `tests_added`, `scope_drift_severity_by_phase`\n  - `receipts_ref` \u2014 relative path list to the receipts that belong to this profile\n- `judge_verdict`: `{ judge_model, rank[], rationale_per_profile, scope_drift_flags, concerns[] }`\n- `human_decision`: `{ chosen_profile, rationale, decided_at }` (populated by `bakeoff pick`)\n\n### 5. Merge mechanics (asymmetric)\n\n`megaplan bakeoff merge --exp <id>` applies:\n\n- **Code half** (from one chosen worktree): `git diff <base-sha>..<chosen-worktree-HEAD>` piped to `git apply` in the main tree. Not `git merge` \u2014 we don't want throwaway-branch commit history. `git apply` fails loudly on conflicts with any main-tree work done in parallel, which is the right behavior.\n- **Evaluation half** (from *all* worktrees): copy `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/<profile>/` trees back. Paths are profile-namespaced by construction \u2014 no collision.\n- **Chosen plan dir**: the winning profile's `.megaplan/plans/<plan-id>/` is copied into the main tree's `.megaplan/plans/` so follow-up work can reference it. Losing profiles' plan dirs remain only in the bake-off archive, not in the live plan list.\n\nGotcha to solve explicitly: `state.json.config.project_dir` contains absolute paths from the worktree. The merge step either rewrites those paths to the main-tree path or marks archived plan dirs as read-only forensic data that must not be loaded by live commands.\n\n### 6. Failure & partial success\n\n- Each profile exits with a `DriverOutcome.status` in `{done, stalled, escalated, failed, aborted, cap, blocked, awaiting_human, tiebreaker_*}`.\n- The bake-off treats anything other than `done` as a non-winner candidate but **still surfaces it to compare** \u2014 a profile stalling at gate is valuable evaluation data (\"this profile can't plan this kind of idea\").\n- `megaplan bakeoff resume <exp-id>` checks each profile's state, relaunches auto only for profiles that haven't reached terminal, leaves winners alone.\n\n### 7. CLI surface\n\n```\nmegaplan bakeoff run --idea-file <path> --profiles A B C [--mode code] [--exp-id <name>]\nmegaplan bakeoff status [--exp <id>]\nmegaplan bakeoff tail --exp <id> [--profile X]\nmegaplan bakeoff compare --exp <id> [--judge <model>]\nmegaplan bakeoff pick --exp <id> --profile <name> [--rationale <text>]\nmegaplan bakeoff merge --exp <id>\nmegaplan bakeoff resume --exp <id>\nmegaplan bakeoff abandon --exp <id>\n```\n\n### 8. State\n\nOne top-level bake-off state at `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/bakeoff.json`:\n- `experiment_id`, `base_sha`, `idea_hash`, `idea_path`\n- `profiles[].{name, worktree, plan_id, pid, launched_at, outcome}`\n- `chosen_profile`, `merged_at`, `phase` (running / compared / picked / merged / abandoned)\n\nThis is a thin coordination layer. The per-profile `state.json` inside each worktree remains the authoritative per-run record.\n\n### 9. Out of scope\n\n- LLM-judge prompt-calibration fixtures and human-calibration set (Sprint 3 or later).\n- Isolated-phase replay (enabled by Sprint 1 canonical hashes, but separate UX).\n- Automatic judge scheduling post-run (keep `bakeoff compare` explicit for v1 \u2014 auto-running doubles cost for rarely-queried data).\n\n## Success criteria\n\n1. `megaplan bakeoff run --idea-file foo.md --profiles standard all-open all-kimi` launches three concurrent autos, each in its own worktree, with independent per-profile logs.\n2. `bakeoff status` shows an accurate live table while runs are in flight.\n3. After all profiles reach terminal state, `bakeoff compare` produces a `comparison.json` conforming to the documented schema \u2014 with receipts-based metrics for every completed profile and graceful nulls for failed profiles.\n4. `bakeoff pick` records a human selection; `bakeoff merge` applies exactly the chosen profile's diff to the main tree and copies all profiles' audit data into `.megaplan/bakeoffs/<exp-id>/`.\n5. Crashing one profile mid-run does not prevent the others from completing or the comparison from running.\n6. A resumed bake-off (`bakeoff resume`) does not re-run already-completed profiles.\n7. Worktrees are fully removed after a successful merge; retained (with crash marker) after a crash.",
